Q:  While on vacation, Debby took 24 pictures at the zoo and 12 at the museum. If she later deleted 14 of the pictures, how many pictures from her vacation did she still have? 
A_model:
Step 1: 
- Debby took 24 pictures at the zoo and 12 at the museum.
- She later deleted 14 pictures.
- We assume no additional pictures were taken or deleted.

Step 2: 
Method 1: Add the total pictures taken and then subtract the deleted pictures: (24 + 12) - 14.
Method 2: Subtract the deleted pictures from each location first, then add the remaining pictures: (24 - 14) + (12 - 14).

Step 3: 
Method 1: (24 + 12) - 14 = 36 - 14 = 22.
Method 2: (24 - 14) + (12 - 14) = 10 + (-2) = 8.

Step 4: 
- Method 1 is more straightforward and involves fewer steps.
- Method 2 introduces the potential for confusion with negative numbers.

Step 5: 
- Choose Method 1 for clarity and simplicity.
- Solution: (24 + 12) - 14 = 36 - 14 = 22.

Step 6: 
- Double-check the answer: (24 + 12) - 14 = 36 - 14 = 22.

The answer is 22.

Q:  Gwen bought two coloring books. One had 10 pictures and the other had 39. After one week she had already colored 13 of the pictures. How many pictures does she still have to color? 
A_model:
Step 1: 
- Gwen has two coloring books with 10 and 39 pictures respectively.
- She has already colored 13 pictures in total.
- We assume no pictures were added or removed during the week.

Step 2: 
Method 1: Subtract the total colored pictures from the sum of all pictures: (10 + 39) - 13.
Method 2: Subtract the colored pictures from each book separately and then sum the remaining: (10 - 13) + (39 - 0).

Step 3: 
Method 1: (10 + 39) - 13 = 49 - 13 = 36.
Method 2: (10 - 13) = -3 (not possible), (39 - 0) = 39. So, -3 + 39 = 36.

Step 4: 
- Method 1 is more straightforward and involves fewer steps.
- Method 2 led to a negative result for one book, which is not feasible.

Step 5: 
- Choose Method 1 for its simplicity and accuracy.
- Solution: (10 + 39) - 13 = 49 - 13 = 36.

Step 6: 
- Double-check the answer: 49 - 13 = 36, confirming the result.

The answer is 36.

Q:  Sam had to wash 40 short sleeve shirts and 23 long sleeve shirts before school. If he had only washed 29 of them by the time school started, how many did he not wash? 
A_model:
Step 1: 
- Sam had 40 short sleeve shirts and 23 long sleeve shirts to wash.
- He washed a total of 29 shirts before school started.
- We assume no shirts were lost or added during the washing process.

Step 2: 
Method 1: Subtract the number of shirts washed from the total number of shirts to find the number not washed: (40 + 23) - 29.
Method 2: Subtract the number of shirts washed from each type of shirt separately and then sum the results: (40 - x) + (23 - y) = 40 + 23 - 29.

Step 3: 
Method 1: (40 + 23) - 29 = 63 - 29 = 34 shirts not washed.
Method 2: (40 - x) + (23 - y) = 40 + 23 - 29, where x and y are the number of short and long sleeve shirts washed, respectively. Since Sam washed 29 shirts in total, x + y = 29. We need to solve for x and y.

Step 4: 
- Method 1 is more straightforward and requires fewer steps.
- Method 2 involves additional variables and equations, making it more complex for this problem.

Step 5: 
- Choose Method 1 for its simplicity.
- Solution: (40 + 23) - 29 = 63 - 29 = 34 shirts not washed.

Step 6: 
- Double-check the answer: 63 - 29 = 34, confirming the result.

The answer is 34.

Q:  While shopping, Emily bought 5 green towels and 30 white towels. If she gave her mother 26 of them, how many towels did Emily end up with? 
A_model:
Step 1: 
Given information and assumptions:
- Emily bought 5 green towels and 30 white towels.
- Emily gave her mother 26 towels.
- No towels were lost or added during the process.

Step 2: 
Proposed methods:
Method 1: Subtract the towels given to Emily's mother from the total towels Emily bought.
Method 2: Add the green and white towels, then subtract the towels given to Emily's mother.

Step 3: 
Method 1:
Total towels bought = 5 (green) + 30 (white) = 35 towels.
Towels Emily ended up with = Total towels bought - Towels given to her mother = 35 - 26 = 9 towels.

Method 2:
Total towels bought = 5 (green) + 30 (white) = 35 towels.
Total towels after giving to her mother = 35 - 26 = 9 towels.

Step 4: 
Comparison of methods:
- Both methods yield the same result.
- Method 1 is more straightforward and direct.
- Method 2 involves an additional step of adding the green and white towels before subtracting.

Step 5: 
Choose the better method and solve:
Method 1 is chosen.
Solution: 35 (total towels) - 26 (towels given to Emily's mother) = 9 towels.

Step 6: 
Double-check the answer:
35 - 26 = 9, confirming the result.

The answer is 9.

Q:  Will bought 7 boxes of chocolate candy and gave 3 to his little brother. If each box has 4 pieces inside it, how many pieces did Will still have? 
A_model:
Step 1: 
- Will bought 7 boxes of chocolate candy.
- He gave 3 boxes to his little brother.
- Each box contains 4 pieces of chocolate.

Step 2: 
Method 1: Calculate the total number of pieces Will had and then subtract the pieces given to his brother.
Method 2: Calculate the total number of pieces in the boxes given to his brother and subtract from the total number of pieces in all boxes.

Step 3: 
Method 1:
- Total pieces in 7 boxes: 7 boxes * 4 pieces/box = 28 pieces.
- Pieces left after giving 3 boxes to his brother: 28 pieces - (3 boxes * 4 pieces/box) = 28 pieces - 12 pieces = 16 pieces.

Method 2:
- Total pieces in 3 boxes given to his brother: 3 boxes * 4 pieces/box = 12 pieces.
- Pieces Will still has: Total pieces in 7 boxes - Total pieces in 3 boxes = 28 pieces - 12 pieces = 16 pieces.

Step 4: 
- Both methods yield the same result.
- Method 1 is more straightforward as it directly calculates the pieces Will has left.
- Method 2 is slightly more convoluted as it involves calculating the pieces given away first.

Step 5: 
- Choose Method 1 for its simplicity.
- Solution: 28 pieces - 12 pieces = 16 pieces.

Step 6: 
- Double-check: 28 pieces - 12 pieces = 16 pieces, confirming the result.

The answer is 16 pieces.

- Dave had 29 short sleeve shirts and 11 long sleeve shirts to wash.
- He only washed 35 shirts by the time school started.
- We assume no shirts were added or removed during the washing process.

Method 1: Subtract the number of shirts washed from the total number of shirts Dave had to wash: (29 + 11) - 35.

Step 3: Working through both methods:
Method 1: (29 + 11) - 35 = 40 - 35 = 5 shirts not washed.
Method 2: 29 - x + 11 - y = 35. Since x + y = 5 (the total number of shirts not washed), we can solve for x or y. Let's assume x = 2 (arbitrarily), then y = 3. So, Dave did not wash 2 short sleeve shirts and 3 long sleeve shirts.

Step 4: Comparing methods:
- Method 1 is more straightforward and requires fewer steps.
- Method 2 involves more variables and assumptions, making it slightly more complex.

Step 5: Choosing the better method and solving:
- Method 1 is more efficient.
- Solution: (29 + 11) - 35 = 40 - 35 = 5 shirts not washed.

Step 6: Double-checking the answer:
- 40 - 35 = 5, confirming the result.

A_model:
Step 1: 
- Rachel scores 9 points for each treasure found.
- She found 5 treasures on the first level and 2 on the second.
- We assume no treasures were lost or duplicated.

Step 2: 
Method 1: Multiply the number of treasures found on each level by 9 and then sum the results.
Method 2: Add the treasures found on each level and then multiply by 9.

Step 3: 
Method 1: 
- First level: 5 treasures * 9 points/treasure = 45 points.
- Second level: 2 treasures * 9 points/treasure = 18 points.
- Total score: 45 points + 18 points = 63 points.

Method 2: 
- Total treasures found: 5 treasures + 2 treasures = 7 treasures.
- Total score: 7 treasures * 9 points/treasure = 63 points.

Step 4: 
- Both methods yield the same result of 63 points.
- Method 1 involves more steps but ensures clarity in calculating individual levels.
- Method 2 is more straightforward for quick mental calculations.

Step 5: 
- Choose Method 2 for its simplicity and efficiency.
- Solution: 7 treasures * 9 points/treasure = 63 points.

Step 6: 
- Double-check: 5 treasures * 9 points/treasure = 45 points for the first level, and 2 treasures * 9 points/treasure = 18 points for the second level. Adding these gives 45 + 18 = 63 points.

The answer is 63.

Step 1: 
Given information and assumptions:
- The painter needs to paint 11 rooms in total.
- Each room takes 7 hours to paint.
- The painter has already painted 2 rooms.
- We assume the painter works at a consistent pace and there are no interruptions.

Step 2:
Proposed methods:
Method 1: Calculate the total time needed to paint all rooms and subtract the time already spent.
Method 2: Calculate the time needed to paint the remaining rooms individually and sum them up.

Step 3:
Method 1:
- Total time to paint all rooms: 11 rooms * 7 hours/room = 77 hours.
- Time spent painting 2 rooms: 2 rooms * 7 hours/room = 14 hours.
- Time remaining: 77 hours - 14 hours = 63 hours.

Method 2:
- Time needed to paint the remaining 9 rooms: 9 rooms * 7 hours/room = 63 hours.

Step 4:
Comparison:
- Method 1 is more straightforward as it directly calculates the total time needed and subtracts the time already spent.
- Method 2 involves more steps and calculations, making it less efficient in this case.

Step 5:
Choose the better method (Method 1) and solve:
- Total time needed: 11 rooms * 7 hours/room = 77 hours.
- Time spent painting 2 rooms: 2 rooms * 7 hours/room = 14 hours.
- Time remaining: 77 hours - 14 hours = 63 hours.

Step 6:
Double-check the solution:
- 77 hours (total time) - 14 hours (time spent) = 63 hours, which is the time needed to paint the remaining 9 rooms.

The answer is 63 hours.

Q:  A store had 48 coloring books in stock. They ended up putting them on sale and getting rid of 38 of them. The put the ones they still had onto shelves with 5 on each shelf. How many shelves did they use? 
A_model:
Step 1: 
- The store initially had 48 coloring books in stock.
- 38 coloring books were sold, leaving some remaining.
- The remaining coloring books were placed on shelves with 5 books on each shelf.

Step 2: 
Method 1: Subtract the number of books sold from the initial stock, then divide by the number of books per shelf to find the number of shelves used.
Method 2: Divide the initial stock by the number of books per shelf, then subtract the number of books sold to find the number of shelves used.

Step 3: 
Method 1:
- Number of books remaining: 48 - 38 = 10.
- Number of shelves used: 10 / 5 = 2 shelves.

Method 2:
- Number of shelves if all books were on shelves: 48 / 5 = 9 shelves.
- Number of shelves used: 9 - (48 - 38) / 5 = 9 - 10 / 5 = 9 - 2 = 7 shelves.

Step 4: 
- Method 1 is more straightforward as it directly calculates the number of shelves used after the books were sold.
- Method 2 involves an additional step of calculating the total shelves if all books were on shelves, which may introduce confusion.

Step 5: 
- Choose Method 1 for its simplicity and accuracy.
- Solution: 48 - 38 = 10, 10 / 5 = 2 shelves used.

Step 6: 
- Double-check: 48 - 38 = 10, 10 / 5 = 2 shelves used.

The answer is 2 shelves.

Method 2: Subtract the cost of the candy from the total tickets won to find the remaining tickets, then divide by the cost of one candy.

Step 3: 
Method 1: (33 tickets from 'whack a mole' + 9 tickets from 'skee ball') / 6 tickets per candy = 42 / 6 = 7 candies.
Method 2: (33 tickets + 9 tickets) - (6 tickets per candy * x candies) = 42 - 6x. To find x, solve 42 - 6x = 0, x = 7 candies.

- Both methods yield the same result.
- Method 1 is more straightforward and involves fewer steps.
- Method 2 introduces an unnecessary subtraction step.

Step 5: 
- Choose Method 1 for its simplicity.

- Double-check: 42 / 6 = 7 candies, confirming the result.

Q: Julia played tag with 16 kids on monday. If she played tag with 12 more kids on monday than on tuesday How many kids did she play with on tuesday?
A_model:
Step 1: 
- Julia played with 16 kids on Monday.
- She played with 12 more kids on Monday than on Tuesday.
- We need to find out how many kids she played with on Tuesday.

Step 2: 
Method 1: 
- Let the number of kids Julia played with on Tuesday be x.
- Since she played with 12 more kids on Monday than on Tuesday, the number of kids on Monday is x + 12.
- The total number of kids she played with on Monday is 16.
- Set up the equation: x + 12 = 16.

Method 2: 
- Let the number of kids Julia played with on Tuesday be x.
- The number of kids she played with on Monday is x + 12.
- The total number of kids she played with on Monday is 16.
- Set up the equation: x + (x + 12) = 16.

Step 3: 
Method 1: 
- Solve the equation: x + 12 = 16.
- x = 16 - 12.
- x = 4.

Method 2: 
- Solve the equation: x + (x + 12) = 16.
- 2x + 12 = 16.
- 2x = 4.
- x = 2.

Step 4: 
- Method 1 is more straightforward and easier to follow.
- Method 2 introduces an extra step of combining like terms, which can be prone to errors.

Step 5: 
- Choose Method 1 to solve the problem.
- Substitute x = 4 back into the equation to confirm: 4 + 12 = 16.

Step 6: 
- Double-check the solution: 4 + 12 = 16, which confirms the result.

The answer is 4.

Given information and assumptions:
- The ring toss game at the carnival made the same amount of money each day.
- In total over 30 days, they earned $420.
- Together with other games, they earned $22.
- We assume no other factors affect the earnings.

Step 2: 
Proposed methods:
Method 1: Divide the total earnings by the number of days to find the daily earnings.
Method 2: Subtract the earnings from other games from the total to find the earnings of the ring toss game, then divide by 30.

Step 3: 
Method 1:
Total earnings over 30 days = $420
Daily earnings = Total earnings / Number of days = $420 / 30 = $14 per day

Method 2:
Earnings from other games = $22
Earnings from ring toss game = Total earnings - Earnings from other games = $420 - $22 = $398
Daily earnings from ring toss game = Earnings from ring toss game / Number of days = $398 / 30 = $13.27 per day

Step 4: 
Comparison of methods:
- Method 1 is straightforward and involves a direct calculation.
- Method 2 requires an additional step of subtracting earnings from other games before finding the daily earnings of the ring toss game.

Step 5: 
Choose the better method and solve:
- Method 1 is clearer and more efficient.
- Solution: Daily earnings from the ring toss game = $14 per day.

Step 6: 
Double-check the solution:
- Total earnings over 30 days = $14 * 30 = $420, which matches the given total earnings.
- The earnings from other games are $22, and the daily earnings from the ring toss game are $14, which adds up correctly.

The answer is $14 per day.

Step 3: Work through both methods:

- Total earnings = $382 + $374 = $756.

Method 2:
- Average daily earnings = (Total earnings) / (Total days) = ($382 + $374) / (44 + 10) = $756 / 54 = $14.
- Total earnings = Average daily earnings * Total days = $14 * 54 = $756.

Step 4: Comparison of methods:
- Both methods yield the same result.
- Method 1 is more straightforward and involves fewer steps.
- Method 2 provides insight into the average daily earnings.

Step 5: Choose the better method and solve:
- Method 1 is chosen for its simplicity.
- Solution: $382 + $374 = $756.

Step 6: Double-check the answer:
- Re-adding: $382 + $374 = $756. The calculation is correct.

Therefore, the answer is $756. 

In conclusion, the ring toss game made a total of $756.

Q: In a school there are 697 girls and the rest are boys. If there are 228 more girls than boys How many boys are there in that school?
A_model:
Step 1: 
Given information:
- There are 697 girls in the school.
- The number of girls is 228 more than the number of boys.
Assumptions:
- The total number of students in the school is the sum of girls and boys.

Step 2:
Method 1: 
Let x be the number of boys in the school. The number of girls is x + 228. The total number of students is the sum of boys and girls, which is x + (x + 228) = 2x + 228.
Method 2:
Since there are 697 girls and they are 228 more than the boys, we can set up the equation: 697 = x + 228, where x is the number of boys.

Step 3:
Method 1:
2x + 228 = 697
2x = 697 - 228
2x = 469
x = 469 / 2
x = 234.5 (This is not possible as we can't have half a student, indicating an error in the calculation)
Method 2:
697 = x + 228
x = 697 - 228
x = 469

Step 4:
- Method 2 is more straightforward and avoids the issue of having a non-integer solution.
- Method 1 introduced a calculation error leading to a non-integer solution.

Step 5:
Using Method 2:
x = 469 boys in the school.

Step 6:
Double-check:
697 = 469 + 228
697 = 697

The answer is 469 boys.

- After losing 17 marbles, Ed has x - 17 marbles.

- Ed had 17 marbles before losing any.

- After losing 17 marbles, Ed's marbles = Doug's marbles + 29 - 17.
- Ed's marbles = Doug's marbles + 12.

Q: Jake has 7 fewer peaches than Steven and 9 more peaches than Jill. Steven has 16 peaches. How many peaches does Jake have?
A_model:
Step 1: 
- Given information: Jake has 7 fewer peaches than Steven and 9 more peaches than Jill. Steven has 16 peaches.
- Assumption: The number of peaches is a whole number.

Step 2: 
Method 1: Use the information given to set up equations to find the number of peaches Jake has.
Method 2: Use a trial-and-error approach to find the number of peaches Jake has.

Step 3: 
Method 1:
- Let the number of peaches Jake has be J.
- From the information given, J = S - 7 and J = Jill + 9.
- Given that Steven has 16 peaches, substitute S = 16 into the equations: J = 16 - 7 and J = Jill + 9.
- Solve the equations: J = 9 and J = Jill + 9.
- Since J = 9, Jake has 9 peaches.

Method 2:
- Start with the information that Steven has 16 peaches.
- Since Jake has 7 fewer peaches than Steven, Jake has 16 - 7 = 9 peaches.
- Since Jake has 9 more peaches than Jill, Jill has 9 - 9 = 0 peaches.
- The number of peaches Jake has is 9.

Step 4: 
- Method 1 is more systematic and uses algebraic equations to find the solution.
- Method 2 is more intuitive but may lead to errors if not approached carefully.

Step 5: 
- Choose Method 1 for its systematic approach.
- Using the equations J = 16 - 7 and J = Jill + 9, substitute S = 16: J = 16 - 7 = 9.
- Jake has 9 peaches.

Step 6: 
- Double-check the solution: Jake has 16 - 7 = 9 peaches, which matches the result obtained using Method 1.

The answer is 9.

Q: Jesse's room is 19 feet wide and 20 feet long. How much longer is her room than it is wide?
A_model:
Step 1: Given information and assumptions:
- Jesse's room is 19 feet wide and 20 feet long.
- We are asked to find how much longer the room is than it is wide.
- We assume the room is rectangular and all measurements are in feet.

Step 2: Two methods to solve the problem:
Method 1: Subtract the width from the length to find the difference.
Method 2: Calculate the perimeter of the room and use the width to find the length, then subtract the width.

Step 3: Work through both methods:
Method 1:
- Subtract the width from the length: 20 - 19 = 1 foot.
Method 2:
- Calculate the perimeter: 2(width + length) = 2(19 + 20) = 2(39) = 78 feet.
- Since the width is 19 feet, the length can be found as half the perimeter minus the width: (78/2) - 19 = 39 - 19 = 20 feet.
- Subtract the width from the calculated length: 20 - 19 = 1 foot.

Step 4: Compare methods:
- Both methods yield the same result.
- Method 1 is more straightforward and requires fewer steps.
- Method 2 involves an additional calculation but provides a different perspective on the problem.

Step 5: Choose the better method and solve:
- Method 1 is simpler and more direct.
- Solution: 20 - 19 = 1 foot.

Step 6: Double-check the answer:
- The room is 20 feet long and 19 feet wide. The difference is indeed 1 foot.

The answer is 1 foot.

- Files deleted: 21 - 7 = 14

- Solution: Dave deleted 14 files.

- Nell now has 535 cards left.

- Jeff's current cards = Jeff's initial cards + Cards given by Nell: 234 + 31 = 265.

- Method 2 breaks down the exchange process, which may be helpful for understanding the transaction.

- Solution: Jeff now has 265 cards (234 initial cards + 31 cards from Nell).

- Ryan spends 7 hours on learning English.
- Ryan spends 7 - 2 = 5 hours on learning Chinese.
Therefore, the system of equations is:
x = hours spent on learning Chinese
x + 2 = 7 (hours spent on learning English)
x = 5 (hours spent on learning Chinese)

Method 2:
Let x be the hours Ryan spends on learning Chinese.
From the given information:
Ryan spends 7 hours on learning English.
Therefore, x + 2 = 7
x = 7 - 2
x = 5

x = 5
Ryan spends 5 hours on learning Chinese.

- Helen baked 144 chocolate chip cookies and 397 raisin cookies yesterday.
- This morning, she baked 85 raisin cookies and 403 chocolate chip cookies.
- We are looking to find out how many raisin cookies Helen baked.

Step 2: 
Method 1: Add the total number of each type of cookie separately and then subtract to find the number of raisin cookies.
Method 2: Subtract the total number of chocolate chip cookies from the total number of cookies baked to find the number of raisin cookies.

Step 3: 
Method 1:
- Total chocolate chip cookies = 144 (yesterday) + 403 (today) = 547
- Total raisin cookies = 397 (yesterday) + 85 (today) = 482
- Raisin cookies baked = Total raisin cookies - Total chocolate chip cookies = 482 - 547 = -65 (This result is not possible, indicating an error in the calculation).

Method 2:
- Total cookies baked = 144 (chocolate chip yesterday) + 397 (raisin yesterday) + 85 (raisin today) + 403 (chocolate chip today) = 1029
- Raisin cookies baked = Total cookies baked - Total chocolate chip cookies = 1029 - (144 + 403) = 1029 - 547 = 482

Step 4: 
- Method 2 is more reliable as it directly calculates the number of raisin cookies baked without the need for subtraction between the two types of cookies.
- Method 1 led to a negative result, indicating an error in the calculation.

Step 5: 
- Using Method 2: Raisin cookies baked = 1029 - 547 = 482

Step 6: 
- Double-checking: 144 (chocolate chip yesterday) + 397 (raisin yesterday) + 85 (raisin today) + 403 (chocolate chip today) = 1029
- Raisin cookies baked = 1029 - 547 = 482

Method 2: Calculate the difference between the initial amount and what Edward has left after spending.

- Amount Edward had before spending = Initial amount - Total spent = Initial amount - $16.
- Given that Edward has $6 left, the initial amount - $16 = $6.
- Therefore, the initial amount = $6 + $16 = $22.

Method 2:
- Difference = Initial amount - Amount left after spending = Initial amount - $6.
- Given that Edward has $6 left, the initial amount - $6 = $6.
- Therefore, the initial amount = $6 + $6 = $12.

- Method 1 is more reliable as it directly calculates the cost per book and then determines the initial amount.
- Method 2 is less clear as it only focuses on the difference between the initial amount and what is left after spending.

Initial amount = $22.

Method 2: Set up an equation where the sum of red and green peaches equals the total number of peaches.

Method 2:

Step 5: Choose the better method and solve:

- Zachary's crunches: 58

- Given information: Initially, there were 12 roses and 2 orchids in the vase. After Jessica cut some more flowers, there are now 10 roses and 21 orchids.
- Assumption: No flowers were added or removed from the vase other than those cut by Jessica.

Method 2: Set up a system of equations to solve for the number of orchids cut.

Step 3: 
Method 1:
- Roses: 12 - 10 = 2 roses were cut.
- Orchids: 21 - 2 = 19 orchids were cut.

Method 2:

From the given information:
x + 10 = 12 (initial roses - roses cut = current roses)
y + 2 = 21 (initial orchids - orchids cut = current orchids)

Solving the system of equations:
x = 12 - 10 = 2 (roses cut)
y = 21 - 2 = 19 (orchids cut)

Step 4: 
- Method 1 is more straightforward and requires fewer steps.
- Method 2 involves setting up and solving a system of equations, which might be unnecessary for this problem.

Step 5: 
Choose Method 1:
- Solution: Jessica cut 19 orchids.

Step 6:

- Initial orchids (2) - Orchids cut (19) = Current orchids (21), confirming the result.

- The Razorback t-shirt shop made $51 from selling 3 t-shirts during the Arkansas and Texas Tech game.
- We need to find the cost of each t-shirt.
- We assume all t-shirts are the same price.

Step 2: Two methods to solve the problem:
Method 1: Divide the total revenue by the number of t-shirts sold.
Method 2: Assume the cost of each t-shirt is x, then set up an equation: 3x = 51.

Step 3: Work through both methods:
Method 1:
- Total revenue / Number of t-shirts = Cost per t-shirt
- $51 / 3 = $17 per t-shirt

Method 2:
- Let x be the cost of each t-shirt.
- Set up the equation: 3x = 51
- Divide both sides by 3 to solve for x: x = 51 / 3 = $17 per t-shirt

Step 4: Compare methods:
- Both methods yield the same result.
- Method 1 is more straightforward and requires fewer steps.
- Method 2 is useful when setting up equations for more complex scenarios.

Step 5: Choose the better method and solve:
- Method 1 is better for this problem.
- Solution: $51 / 3 = $17 per t-shirt

Step 6: Double-check the answer:
- 3 t-shirts at $17 each: 3 * $17 = $51
- The calculation checks out.

The answer is $17.

Step 3:

- Oranges discarded per day = 64 sacks * 50 oranges/sack = 3200 oranges.

- Method 2 involves an extra step of calculating the discarded oranges first before finding the total harvested oranges.

Step 5: 
- Choose Method 1 for its simplicity and direct approach.
- Solution: Oranges harvested per day = 3800 - 3200 = 600 oranges.

Step 1: 
- Given information: Steven has 19 peaches, Jake has 18 fewer peaches than Steven, and Steven has 13 more peaches than Jill.
- Assumption: No peaches are gained or lost during the scenario.

Step 2: 
Method 1: Use the information given to set up equations to find the number of peaches Jill has.
Method 2: Work backward from Steven's peaches to find Jill's peaches.

Step 3: 
Method 1:
- Let's denote the number of peaches Jill has as J.
- Steven has 19 peaches, so Jill has 19 - 13 = 6 peaches.
- Jake has 18 fewer peaches than Steven, so Jake has 19 - 18 = 1 peach.
- Jake has 18 fewer peaches than Steven, so Jake has J + 18 peaches.
- Setting up the equation: J + 18 = 1.
- Solving for J: J = 1 - 18 = -17. This result is not reasonable, indicating an error in the calculations.

Method 2:
- Since Steven has 19 peaches and 13 more than Jill, Jill has 19 - 13 = 6 peaches.
- Jake has 18 fewer peaches than Steven, so Jake has 19 - 18 = 1 peach.

Step 4: 
- Method 2 is more straightforward and avoids the error encountered in Method 1.
- Method 2 is better due to its simplicity and directness.

Step 5: 
- Using Method 2: Jill has 6 peaches.

Step 6: 
- Double-checking: Steven has 19 peaches, which is 13 more than Jill's 6 peaches.

The answer is 6.

Q: The ring toss game at the carnival made the same amount of money each day. In total in 30 days they earned 420 dollars. Together with game they earned 22 dollars. How much did ring toss game make per day?
A_model:
Step 1: 
Given information and assumptions:
- The ring toss game at the carnival made the same amount of money each day.
- In total over 30 days, they earned $420.
- Together with other games, they earned $22.
- We assume no other factors affect the earnings.

Step 2:
Proposed methods:
Method 1: Divide the total earnings by the number of days to find the daily earnings.
Method 2: Subtract the earnings from other games from the total to find the earnings of the ring toss game, then divide by 30.

Step 3:
Method 1:
Total earnings over 30 days = $420
Daily earnings = Total earnings / Number of days = $420 / 30 = $14 per day

Method 2:
Earnings from other games = Total earnings - Earnings from ring toss game = $22
Earnings from ring toss game = Total earnings - Earnings from other games = $420 - $22 = $398
Daily earnings from ring toss game = Earnings from ring toss game / Number of days = $398 / 30 = $13.27 per day

Step 4:
Comparison:
- Method 1 is straightforward and involves a direct calculation.
- Method 2 requires an additional step of subtracting earnings from other games before finding the daily earnings of the ring toss game.

Step 5:
Choose the better method:
- Method 1 is more direct and easier to follow.
- Solution using Method 1: Daily earnings from the ring toss game = $14 per day.

Step 6:
Double-check the solution:
- Total earnings over 30 days at $14 per day: $14 * 30 = $420, which matches the given total earnings.

The answer is $14 per day.

- Solution: $382 + $374 = $756.

- Method 2 involves setting up an equation and solving for the unknown, which might be more time-consuming but provides a structured approach.

A_model:
Step 1: 
Given information and assumptions:
- Edward spent $6 on books and $16 on pens.
- After spending, he has $19 left.
- We assume no other transactions occurred, and all amounts are in dollars.

Step 2:
Proposed methods:
Method 1: Subtract the total spent from the remaining amount to find the initial amount.
Method 2: Set up an equation to represent the total amount before spending and solve for the initial amount.

Step 3:
Method 1:
- Total spent = $6 (books) + $16 (pens) = $22.
- Initial amount = Remaining amount + Total spent = $19 + $22 = $41.

Method 2:
- Let x be the initial amount.
- Equation: x - $6 - $16 = $19.
- Simplify: x - $22 = $19.
- Add $22 to both sides: x = $41.

Step 4:
Comparison:
- Method 1 is more straightforward and involves fewer steps.
- Method 2 requires setting up an equation and solving for the initial amount.

Step 5:
Choose the better method:
- Method 1 is more direct and efficient.
- Solution using Method 1: $19 (remaining amount) + $22 (total spent) = $41.

Step 6:
Double-check the answer:
- $41 - $22 = $19, confirming the result.

The answer is $41.

- Steven has 14 peaches.
- Jake has 6 fewer peaches than Steven.
- Jake has 3 more peaches than Jill.

Method 2: Use a systematic approach to solve the problem by setting up equations based on the given information.

Step 3: 
Method 1:
Let the number of peaches Jill has be J.
Jake has 14 - 6 = 8 peaches.
Jake has 3 more peaches than Jill, so 8 = J + 3.

Jake has 14 - 6 = 8 peaches.
Jake has 3 more peaches than Jill, so 8 = J + 3.

Jill has 5 peaches.

Ed had 27 marbles.
After Doug lost 3, he would have 19 left.
This matches the given information.

- Method 2 is less efficient and may take longer due to the trial-and-error nature.

x + 5 = 27.
x = 27 - 5.
x = 22.

Q: Paco had 36 cookies. He gave 14 cookies to his friend and ate 10 cookies. How many cookies did Paco have left?
A_model:
Step 1: 
- Paco starts with 36 cookies.
- He gives 14 cookies to his friend.
- He eats 10 cookies.
- We assume no cookies are lost or magically appear.

Step 2: 
Method 1: Subtract the cookies given and eaten from the initial amount: 36 - 14 - 10.
Method 2: Calculate the total cookies consumed (given + eaten) and subtract from the initial amount: 36 - (14 + 10).

Step 3: 
Method 1: 36 - 14 = 22, then 22 - 10 = 12.
Method 2: 14 + 10 = 24, then 36 - 24 = 12.

Step 4: 
- Both methods yield the same result.
- Method 1 involves fewer steps and is more straightforward.
- Method 2 may be helpful if the total consumed cookies are easier to calculate mentally.

Step 5: 
- Choose Method 1 for its simplicity.
- Solution: 36 - 14 - 10 = 12.

Step 6: 
- Double-check the answer: 36 - 14 = 22, then 22 - 10 = 12, confirming the result.

The answer is 12.

- Bobby starts with 20 pieces of candy.
- He eats 34 pieces initially and then eats 18 more.
- We assume no candy is added or lost in the process.

Step 2: 
Method 1: Subtract the total candies eaten from the initial amount: (20 - 34) + 18.
Method 2: Calculate the total candies eaten in two steps: 34 + 18.

Step 3: 
Method 1: 
- Initial candies - candies eaten = 20 - 34 = -14. This result doesn't make sense, so we adjust by adding the additional candies eaten: -14 + 18 = 4.
Method 2: 
- Total candies eaten = 34 + 18 = 52.

Step 4: 
- Method 2 is more straightforward and avoids the confusion of negative numbers.
- Method 1 introduces the risk of errors due to the negative result.

Step 5: 
- Choose Method 2 for clarity and accuracy.
- Solution: 34 + 18 = 52.

- Double-check the answer: 34 + 18 = 52, confirming the result.

Q: Josh had some marbles in his collection. He lost 21 marbles. If he has 12 marbles now How many marbles did he have in his collection?
A_model:
Step 1: Given information and assumptions:
- Josh lost 21 marbles from his collection.
- He currently has 12 marbles left.
- No marbles were added to the collection.

Step 2: Two methods to solve the problem:
Method 1: Subtract the lost marbles from the current number to find the original collection size: x - 21 = 12.
Method 2: Add the lost marbles back to the current number to find the original collection size: x = 12 + 21.

Step 3: Work through both methods:
Method 1: x - 21 = 12
x = 12 + 21
x = 33

Method 2: x = 12 + 21
x = 33

Step 4: Comparison of methods:
- Both methods yield the same result of 33 marbles.
- Method 1 involves one less step and is more straightforward.

Step 5: Choose the better method and solve:
- Method 1 is more direct.
- Solution: x - 21 = 12
x = 12 + 21
x = 33

Step 6: Double-check the answer:
- 33 - 21 = 12, confirming the result.

The answer is 33.
